# NEXA MAIN — PERMANENT INTERNAL MASTER ROADMAP

**Authority:** This file (`MASTER_ROADMAP.md`) is the single authoritative, in-app execution roadmap for NEXA Main. Historical external PDFs and old temporary/priority roadmaps are reference-only archives and cannot override this file. Do not create competing active roadmap files. Keep the existing historical milestone records below intact; update statuses only with source/test evidence.

**Execution contract:** Each run selects one ordered block of exactly 25 milestones. Work in dependency-aware slices, but keep the block definition at 25. For every milestone use CHECK → FIX → TEST → VERIFY → DOCUMENT. Record `TESTED/PASSED`, `FAIL`, `BLOCKED`, `DEFERRED`, or `NOT TESTED` honestly. A milestone is not complete because it is planned or because a related feature exists. External credentials, provider approval, production systems, device/store access, and live integrations stay `NOT TESTED` unless actually exercised. Never bypass server-side authorization, ownership checks, privacy boundaries, or the locked version/release rules in `AGENTS.md`.

**Current forward queue:** M7516–M7765, ten sequential blocks × 25 milestones from the active pointer. M7491–M7515 is retained as completed history; the active block M7516–M7540 remains open because M7527 requires runtime HTTP/SQLite failure-injection evidence. Later registrations are plans, not completion evidence. After each run, update this file and a separate dated execution report. Replan only by updating this master in a reviewed change, preserving historical IDs and evidence.

## B143 / V5.98.5 — Pending-payment reconciler (patch release) — IMPLEMENTED / VALIDATED
- Current release: **V5.98.5 / B143**. Follows V5.98.4 / B142.
- Added `server/paymentReconciler.js`: a background job (every 5 minutes, live Paystack mode only, kill switch `PAYMENT_RECONCILE_DISABLED=1`) that re-verifies Kitchen and Yaayz orders still `pending_payment` after 15 minutes (window 48 hours) and confirms those Paystack reports as paid, using the same reference/currency/amount matcher and the same guarded UPDATE as the webhook. Cancelled orders are never reopened; paid-after-cancellation and mismatches are recorded with the existing outcomes so the V5.98.4 attention report lists them. Checks back off and give up after 6 attempts. No schema change.
- Not tested against live Paystack (needs a live account); behaviour covered by 13 tests using a fake provider and a real SQLite database.
- Batch advanced **B142 → B143** after audit → repair → test → release.
- Next block: **M1825–M1849 / B144**.

## B142 / V5.98.4 — Payment attention report (patch release) — IMPLEMENTED / VALIDATED
- Current release: **V5.98.4 / B142**. Follows V5.98.3 / B141.
- Added a read-only, administrator-only `GET /api/admin/payments/attention` report (`server/paymentAttention.js`) that surfaces payments the webhook could not apply: paid after cancellation (mall, Yaayz, kitchen), payment without an order, amount/status mismatches, failed vendor activation, refunds without a record, and webhook events stuck unfinished. Existing webhook behaviour is unchanged. See V5_98_4_REPORT.md.
- Batch advanced **B141 → B142** after audit → repair → test → release.
- Next block: **M1825–M1849 / B143**.

## V5.48.0 — Batch B85 / Navigation Shell Stabilization & Quick Search Rail — IMPLEMENTED / VALIDATED
### Batch B85 — 10 milestones (historical batch; new standard begins B86)
565. **Five-world user dock boundary: remove Profile/Settings from the global bottom dock** — IMPLEMENTED
566. **Preserve Profile/Settings as a real deep-link route outside the world dock** — IMPLEMENTED
567. **Strict user/vendor world separation for Money, Zeeza, Marketplace, Yaayz and Leisure** — IMPLEMENTED
568. **Preserve Zeeza Inbox/Friends/Chase as an internal Zeeza navigation surface** — IMPLEMENTED
569. **Preserve Marketplace vendor/admin entry inside the Marketplace world boundary** — IMPLEMENTED
570. **Home cinematic Quick Search Rail for universal NEXA search access** — IMPLEMENTED
571. **Preserve global inner-screen Search key and dedicated Search route** — IMPLEMENTED
572. **Preserve directory, notification, Add and search-result deep-link routing** — IMPLEMENTED
573. **Navigation shell accessibility, motion and enlarged-text regression protection** — IMPLEMENTED
574. **B85 cumulative navigation regression, metadata, audit and package certification** — VALIDATED

B85 is a navigation stabilization batch, not a product-world rewrite. The global bottom dock now contains only the five user-facing worlds. Profile/settings remains reachable through the existing Home directory and direct routes but no longer consumes a world-dock slot. Home gains a third cinematic rail dedicated to quick NEXA search, while inner screens retain their existing Search key and specialized local search controls. No new business, payment, notification, ticket, vendor, admin or advertising authority is introduced.

# NEXA Master Roadmap

**Canonical roadmap:** the authoritative long-range product map, architecture direction, milestone history, and roadmap governance document.

**Current implemented release:** V5.49.0 — B86 Navigation & UX Integrity Audit

## Roadmap governance

### B87 closure evidence
- The existing account lifecycle backend was audited rather than rebuilt.
- Profile was expanded to expose server-backed privacy/discovery controls, account data export, security summary, lifecycle readiness and password-confirmed account closure.
- Commerce/payment retention, Ajo inventory release, social/call cleanup, session revocation, closed-account denial, recovery expiry and retention bounds were preserved and contract-tested.
- Focused B87 contracts: **25/25 PASS**.
- Full cumulative server suite: **1,315/1,315 PASS**.
- Release audit: **PASS — 5.50.0**.
- Frontend typecheck/device runtime: **NOT RUN**.
- B87 complete cumulative release archive is the authoritative sealed artifact.


**Permanent batch standard:** Starting with B86, every new roadmap execution block contains exactly **25 milestones**. The number 25 means one complete coherent execution block, not 25 changes that must be performed blindly in one pass. Each milestone is implemented, tested, audited, and checkpointed incrementally; discovered defects are fixed before advancing.

**When an AI is told to “check the roadmap(s)”**, it means: read `MASTER_ROADMAP.md`, `PRIORITY_ROADMAP.md`, and `TEMPORARY_ROADMAP.md`; identify the active 25-milestone block; inspect the actual cumulative source and dependencies; reconcile conflicts/stale statuses; continue the next safe incomplete milestone; validate each milestone; and maintain checkpoints. It does **not** mean merely summarize the files or invent a new roadmap.

NEXA uses exactly three active roadmap documents. Starting after B85, all new execution batches contain exactly 25 numbered milestones. Older 5- and 10-milestone batches are historical records and must not be rewritten merely to match the new batch size. The active 25-milestone block is declared in PRIORITY_ROADMAP.md and expanded into actionable work in TEMPORARY_ROADMAP.md.
1. `MASTER_ROADMAP.md` — canonical product map and historical record.
2. `PRIORITY_ROADMAP.md` — the ordered current execution block plus the following queue.
3. `TEMPORARY_ROADMAP.md` — the execution board for the current 25-milestone batch.

No fourth active roadmap may be created. Historical specifications/reports remain evidence, not competing roadmaps.

### AI roadmap-check protocol — authoritative from B86 onward
When an AI is told to “check the roadmaps”, “check the roadmap”, “continue the roadmap”, or equivalent, it must:
1. Read `MASTER_ROADMAP.md`, `PRIORITY_ROADMAP.md`, and `TEMPORARY_ROADMAP.md` before proposing or implementing roadmap work.
2. Treat `MASTER_ROADMAP.md` as the canonical history/product map, `PRIORITY_ROADMAP.md` as the ordered execution queue, and `TEMPORARY_ROADMAP.md` as the active execution board.
3. Treat **25 milestones as exactly one roadmap execution block from B86 onward**. Never silently convert a 25-milestone block into a 5- or 10-milestone block.
4. Preserve all historical milestone numbering and statuses; do not rewrite history to fit the new block size.
5. Inspect the actual cumulative source architecture before changing any milestone. Do not implement from roadmap wording alone.
6. Work incrementally inside the 25-milestone block: each milestone gets its own implementation, regression coverage, and validation boundary. Smaller internal checkpoints are allowed and encouraged.
7. Resolve discovered bugs, regressions, stale contracts, security/authorization issues, or architectural conflicts before advancing past the affected milestone.
8. At the end of milestone 25, run the full cumulative regression suite and release audit, reconcile all roadmap/version/report metadata, and seal the **full cumulative project**, not a patch-only artifact.
9. Never claim runtime, provider, browser, device, dependency, or external-service validation without actual evidence. Record PASS, FAIL, BLOCKED, NOT TESTED, DEFERRED, or CARRY-FORWARD honestly.
10. If a user explicitly changes the batch size later, update this governance contract and all three active roadmap documents before executing under the new rule.

### Status vocabulary
`PLANNED` · `IN PROGRESS` · `IMPLEMENTED` · `VALIDATED` · `BLOCKED` · `DEFERRED` · `CARRY-FORWARD` · `RETIRED`

A feature is not considered validated merely because source code exists. Server tests, client/runtime tests, security/authorization, migration, operational readiness and real-provider checks are recorded separately where applicable.

## Whole-app product map — current reality

### 1. Home / Dami
- Dami is the primary Home/front-door experience.
- Text, recorded voice, live voice, upload/screenshot analysis and Quick ScamCheck are integrated into the Home experience.
- Dami server capabilities include orchestration, language handling, personalization, planning, workflows, AI memory, knowledge, notes, tasks, calendar and voice preferences.
- Future work: durable action confirmations, richer cross-module actions, trustworthy automation boundaries, provider/runtime validation, and production observability.

### 2. Sky Money
- Public product family: Sky Money, Sky Wallet, Sky Savings, Sky Group Savings, Sky Store Savings, Sky Market Watch, Sky Insights.
- Existing backend includes wallet, deposits, withdrawals, peer transfers, transactions/provider status, Ajo/store savings, groups, planning/budgets/goals, market quotes/preferences/insights and financial intelligence.
- Existing Paystack webhook/payment boundaries and fee-preview flows must remain server-authoritative.
- Future work: live provider validation, reconciliation, idempotency/settlement hardening, transaction lifecycle visibility, backup/restore and financial audit operations.

### 3. Zeeza / Chase
- Zeeza owns social identity, profile/media, friends, requests, DMs, rooms, blocks, visibility and share links.
- Chase is the opt-in discovery layer with subscription reach, coarse location, preference filters and server-side stranger-add limits.
- Current safeguards include block checks, consent/terms, pending caps, rate limits, coarse location only and a location-change cooldown.
- Future work: stronger trust/safety, reporting/moderation, age assurance before age-dependent discovery, abuse monitoring and operational tooling.

### 4. Dream Marketplace
- Marketplace supports product discovery, favorites, inquiries/messages, orders/status, reports and existing `/api/mall/*` vendor/admin/payment infrastructure.
- Vendor workflows include registration, products, staff, orders, incomes and disputes.
- Mall/admin workflows include disputes, status, products and payment verification.
- Future work: production payment/provider validation, vendor operations, refunds/disputes lifecycle, media/object storage, delivery/status integrations and operational analytics.

### 5. Yaayz
- Ticket/ads marketplace with event/stadium inventory, ticket purchase, fee preview/retry and payment verification.
- L.A. Ball House and Game House reuse Yaayz rather than creating a second ticket system.
- Future work: verified venue inventory, event-result/leaderboard sources, provider validation, ticket lifecycle reconciliation and venue operations.

### 6. L.A. Experiences
- The Kitchen with menu/order/referral/receipt/cashback/fee-preview/payment flows.
- Ball House and Game House use Yaayz ticket inventory.
- Fitness has approved gym directory/submission/admin review plus bundled guides.
- Future work: real operator inventory, gym approval operations, venue integrations, verified event results, receipts/refunds and safe expansion.

### 7. Identity / Auth / Profile
- Server-side account auth, sessions, logout, multi-session controls and account lifecycle/portability/closure exist.
- Active Expo app has login/signup, AuthProvider/AuthGate, native SecureStore persistence, web memory fallback, expiry handling and protected routes.
- Profile includes account/session controls and existing Zeeza identity continuity.
- Future work: runtime/device validation, recovery hardening, security telemetry and account lifecycle UX completion.

### 8. Notifications / Search / Shared Experience
- Central notifications, read/read-all actions, push registration/unregistration, unified search, favorites and shared event correlation exist.
- Future work: delivery reliability, notification preferences, deep links, deduplication and operational metrics.

### 9. Extensions / Developer Platform
- Extension manifests, developer validation, capabilities, identity/keys, consent, enable/disable, audit and runtime capability boundaries exist.
- Platform contracts and compatibility/version negotiation are established.
- Future work: durable extension jobs, stronger sandbox/resource controls, developer onboarding, signed artifacts, observability and production marketplace/distribution strategy.

### 10. Staff / Admin / Operations
- Staff route, mall admin/vendor staff, disputes, fitness approval and operational readiness modules exist.
- Future work: unified operations console, audit trails, role/permission review, backups/restore, incident/runbook automation and provider reconciliation.

### 11. Platform / Security / Reliability
- Server-authoritative authorization, rate limiting, concurrency tests, idempotency, security adversarial tests, maintenance, Sentry integration hooks and release/builder gates exist.
- Known architecture debt includes large server modules, some process-local state, dependency/runtime validation debt and operational configuration enforcement.
- Future work: durable multi-instance state, persistent queues where needed, DB backup/restore drills, observability, dependency reproducibility and deployment safety.

## Milestone batching policy

Historical 5- and 10-milestone batches remain unchanged historical records. From the next batch after B85 onward, each active roadmap batch must contain **exactly 25 milestones**. Plan them as one coherent system objective, but implement in small dependency-aware subgroups (typically 3–5 milestones), validating after each subgroup. A milestone is not complete merely because code exists: preserve architecture and authorities, add/update regression contracts, run relevant tests, inspect failures, fix and rerun. Close the 25-milestone batch only after cumulative regression, security/authorization review where applicable, release audit, metadata/roadmap reconciliation, implementation report, and a complete cumulative ZIP checkpoint. Do not silently skip, merge, renumber, or mark blocked work complete. If a genuine blocker occurs, document it and continue only with independent safe milestones.

## Long-range program

### Foundation and release engineering
- Auth/bootstrap runtime validation.
- Dependency reproducibility and client build pipeline.
- Production DB path, backups, restore drills, migration safety.
- Unified release/observability gates.

### Financial platform
- Live provider rails and webhook reconciliation.
- Wallet lifecycle and transaction ledger hardening.
- Ajo/group/store savings production operations.
- Market data reliability and user-facing insight quality.

### Trust and social platform
- Chase age/trust assurance.
- Reporting, moderation and abuse operations.
- Privacy/visibility controls and safe discovery.
- Social notification/deep-link reliability.

### Commerce and experiences
- Dream Marketplace vendor lifecycle, disputes, refunds and delivery operations.
- Yaayz verified ticket/event lifecycle.
- L.A. operator inventory, Kitchen/venue/fitness operations.

### AI and Dami
- Durable action confirmation state.
- Safe cross-module actions with authorization.
- AI memory/knowledge/planning/workflow reliability.
- Provider resilience, cost controls and observability.

### Developer ecosystem
- Extension signing, sandboxing, capability governance and lifecycle.
- Developer tooling and distribution.
- Runtime auditability and compatibility.

### Scale and architecture
- Break oversized server modules along stable domain boundaries without a rewrite.
- Durable state for multi-instance execution.
- Queue/job architecture where asynchronous work becomes material.
- Database indexing, migrations, backup and restore discipline.

## Historical record

V3.x through V4.16.0 implementation reports remain the historical evidence of completed work. They are not active competing roadmaps. V4.16.0 delivered the Auth Bootstrap & Session Foundation and is the starting checkpoint for the five-milestone batch program.

## AI roadmap handoff contract

When the user says **“check the roadmaps”**, **“continue the roadmap”**, or equivalent, the AI must:
1. Read `MASTER_ROADMAP.md` first; it is the canonical roadmap authority.
2. Read `PRIORITY_ROADMAP.md` and `TEMPORARY_ROADMAP.md` and reconcile them with the latest sealed version.
3. Inspect the actual cumulative source before proposing or implementing changes; do not rely on roadmap text alone.
4. Identify the latest sealed batch, the active 25-milestone block, and the exact next unfinished milestone.
5. Preserve all working architecture, UI, navigation, authorities, security/ownership boundaries, accessibility and existing business behavior unless a roadmap milestone explicitly changes them.
6. Work incrementally inside the 25-milestone block, using focused tests and checkpoints instead of one uncontrolled bulk rewrite.
7. If an audit finds an issue, fix it before moving forward and rerun the relevant focused and cumulative tests.
8. At the end of milestone 25, run cumulative regression, release audit, documentation reconciliation and full-project packaging.
9. Update version, implementation report, roadmap state and checkpoint artifacts before sealing.
10. Never claim runtime/device validation unless it was actually run; use `TESTED/PASSED`, `NOT TESTED`, `BLOCKED`, or `DEFERRED` accurately.
11. Never create a competing roadmap document unless explicitly instructed.
12. Never erase historical milestone records merely to make the current roadmap look cleaner.

- **Cross-module motion adoption:** STARTED — shared motion primitives are mounted at the app shell and adopted in navigation and Ball House; further module adoption belongs to the next refinement cycle.
- **Validation:** targeted B10 contracts PASS; release audit PASS after metadata synchronization. Final cumulative seal remains open until the full batch validation/reporting gate is reconciled.

### B10 decisions
- Free remains static; motion entitlements are resolved from the authenticated subscription state rather than from client-selected tier values.
- VIP 100% means access to all motion intentionally implemented in NEXA, not constant movement.
- Ball House reads football data through a server boundary and keeps Yaayz as the ticket/payment authority.
- External football data is cached briefly and rate-limited; empty provider failures are not treated as valid football data.
- No Ball House payment or inventory database was duplicated.

### Navigation architecture correction — V4.18.1
The Royal Dark style reference remains visual-only. NEXA retains its canonical seven-section navigation shell; Dami remains the AI companion and Inbox is the user-facing messaging label. See `docs/design/NEXA_NAVIGATION_ARCHITECTURE.md`.


## V4.20.0 — Product Quality, Observability, Extension Ecosystem, Dami & Future Expansion
- Royal Dark is an adaptive system: module content determines layout density while shared tokens determine visual language.
- Accessibility primitives are centralized and reused rather than duplicated per screen.
- Observability extends the existing Sentry/event-correlation foundation; it does not create a competing telemetry path.
- Extension discovery must pass existing governance before publication.
- Dami planning is bounded, confirmation-aware and cross-module capable; Dami remains the AI companion.
- Future product candidates are dependency-gated and are not treated as launched features until runtime validation exists.

## V4.21.0 — Production Readiness, Trust Operations, Commerce Operations & Dami Resilience
- CI now uses `npm ci` as the reproducible-install contract; the missing root lockfile remains an explicit environment debt.
- Sky Money exposes provider-readiness state without implying that configuration is equivalent to settlement.
- Trust/safety reports have normalized escalation semantics while existing social report storage remains authoritative.
- Commerce/experience operations expose pending lifecycle work across existing Marketplace, Yaayz and Kitchen flows.
- Dami provider calls use a centralized bounded timeout/output policy; consequential action fallback remains disabled by default.

## V4.22.0 — Production Runtime, Live Rails, Trust Operations, Fulfillment & Dami Reliability
- Production client validation now has an explicit readiness contract instead of inferred success.
- Live Sky Money requires provider configuration, webhook secret and signature-validation evidence.
- Zeeza moderation exposes normalized review queues while retaining existing report persistence.
- Marketplace, Yaayz and Kitchen gain a shared operational fulfillment summary without replacing domain ownership.
- Dami memory context is deduplicated and bounded before workflow use; current user instructions remain authoritative.
- The canonical seven-section NEXA architecture and user-facing Inbox terminology remain unchanged.

## V4.23.0 — Batch B7 foundation decisions
- Accessibility is a shared contract, not a one-size-fits-all layout.
- Payment reconciliation must verify provider identity and financial fields before applying state.
- Zeeza appeals are durable records separate from authoritative reports.
- Marketplace/Yaayz/L.A. operations aggregate existing source-of-truth records rather than replacing them.
- Dami knowledge is user-owned context only and never an authorization source.


## V4.24.0 — Batch B8 decisions
- Notification and Search now share clearer cross-module discovery semantics without weakening existing privacy boundaries.
- Financial planning gains a read-only period summary derived from existing user-owned records.
- Dream Marketplace discovery adds category/vendor merchandising context while retaining existing product/order source-of-truth flows.
- Zeeza Inbox/Rooms gains a unified activity summary; friendship, block and room membership rules remain authoritative.
- L.A./Yaayz/Kitchen gains a read-oriented discovery surface that connects available experiences without duplicating venue/order data.


## V4.25.0 — Batch B9 decisions
- Notification action completion resolves only server-issued allowlisted destinations and remains authenticated.
- Savings automation creates planning rules and due-date suggestions; it never becomes an autonomous money-moving authority.
- Marketplace personalization and vendor analytics reuse canonical catalog, favorites and inquiries.
- Community room discovery is opt-in per room; member access and social blocking remain authoritative.
- L.A./Yaayz/Kitchen fulfillment summaries read existing order state rather than duplicating payment or inventory state.


## V4.36.0 — Batch B12 decisions
- Marketplace operations read canonical order/dispute state; no duplicate commerce authority.
- Zeeza trust/community summaries preserve authoritative reports, appeals, blocks and room ownership.
- Notification operations expose preference and delivery health without replacing in-app history as source of truth.
- Goal contribution history records user-entered planning progress only; it is not a payment ledger.
- Venue operations aggregate Yaayz/Kitchen lifecycle state; Ball House remains a football/ticket destination, not a second ticket authority.

## V4.41.0 — Batch B13 decisions
- Marketplace resolution is read-oriented; existing payment/refund/dispute modules remain authoritative for mutations.
- Zeeza governance summaries are user-scoped; existing reports, appeals, blocks, room ownership and moderation controls remain authoritative.
- Notification digest/quiet-hour settings are preferences only; in-app notification history remains the source of truth and delivery recovery stays bounded.
- Savings milestones are calculations over user-owned planning data; they never move money or become a payment ledger.
- Event discovery filters canonical Yaayz inventory; Ball House and leisure surfaces do not create a second ticket authority.

## V4.52.0 — Batch B16 decisions
- 1:1 voice/video calls are restricted to authenticated friends and blocked users cannot call each other.
- Call signaling is participant-scoped; NEXA stores session metadata and signaling payloads, not call audio/video media.
- WebRTC carries encrypted media between endpoints; optional TURN is supported for connectivity while remaining outside media decryption.
- Incoming calls use the existing notification system with an allowlisted `call` destination and authenticated call ID.
- Call lifecycle cleanup is integrated with account closure; no Dami or notification system receives private call media.

## V5.98.6 Major Upgrade — 25 Roadmaps

1. Complete the internal architecture map of the current cumulative codebase.
2. Identify duplicated business logic and keep one authoritative owner for each critical domain.
3. Audit shared client state across major modules.
4. Audit client/server responsibility boundaries.
5. Standardize internal error-handling and recovery surfaces.
6. Deep-audit signup, login, logout and session persistence.
7. Audit profile/account state synchronization.
8. Audit user/vendor role boundaries and permission revalidation.
9. Audit account recovery, account-state transitions and protected routes.
10. Strengthen internal authentication failure and recovery UX.
11. Deep-audit wallet state transitions and invariants.
12. Audit financial idempotency and transaction protections.
13. Audit subscription/service-plan state synchronization.
14. Audit critical database writes for partial or contradictory state.
15. Strengthen internal financial error recovery.
16. Deep-audit product creation and editing.
17. Deep-audit variant, stock and reservation synchronization.
18. Audit marketplace user/vendor permissions.
19. Audit Store Ajo end-to-end state transitions.
20. Audit marketplace loading, empty, unavailable, error and success states.
21. Deep visual/UX audit of Home, Royal Dark and Earth Light.
22. Audit typography, spacing and responsive scaling.
23. Audit animation, cinematic background and 3D-layer performance boundaries.
24. Audit Zeeza, Yaayz, Leisure, Money and Marketplace navigation/state continuity.
25. Complete cumulative internal regression and establish the V5.98.6 baseline.

Status: COMPLETE — 25/25 internal areas verified.

## B307 — V5.99.61 — God Hand run 3, part 2 (TypeScript faults found by a stand-in check)
**Theme:** The project typecheck cannot run here (no node_modules), so a strict tsc was run over src/ with the same settings and no React types. Most of its 1,144 error lines are noise from the missing packages. 18 were not, and each was read against the code. Four were real; the rest need a real install to judge.
1. M4526 — Four real faults fixed: (a) GroupScreen Ajo "Record contribution" label read r.name, which the server never sends, so it announced a raw member id; it now uses the member name already in scope. (b) ZeezaInbox row label read t.name and t.username, which DmThread does not have (the other person is t.with), so it always announced the thread id; now t.with.name, then t.with.username, then the id. (c) NexaNavKeys Add panel had an accessibilityLabel that the modalA11y spread right after it always overwrote; the dead one is removed, runtime label unchanged. (d) formAccessibility helpers were untyped and fieldA11y required a second argument that every caller omits; now typed with error optional — FIXED, guard tests PASSED (4 of 4) / NOT TESTED on phone, real typecheck NOT RUN
2. M4527 — The other 14 of the 18 errors (Marketplace cart variant, Yaayz ad boundary, social feed timers, group kind lookup, motion level lookup, Ionicons icon names, tab icons) look like effects of missing React, React Native and Expo types, not code faults, but this cannot be proved without node_modules — OPEN / NOT TESTED (needs npm install and npm run typecheck)

**Not tested:** phone, the real typecheck, Playwright, the 25 environment failures.
**Carry-forward (open work):** M4465 (needs a phone build to show the crash stack), M4490 to M4517 (phone check), M4527 (real typecheck).

### Combined roadmap — NEXA app and admin app, one list (registered B327)
HISTORICAL ONLY. This retired Combined roadmap is preserved for historical milestone evidence. It is no longer an active authority; Main and Admin now use separate roadmaps.
**Lanes.** Every milestone carries one tag: [APP] (the phone app in src/), [ADMIN] (the admin web app in admin-web/), or [BOTH] (the server, or a feature that must work on both sides). The server (server/) is shared, so server work is tagged by who it serves.
**Rules for this list.**
1. A feature is finished only when both sides are. A new feature milestone names its other side, or says "no admin side needed" or "no app side needed".
2. Every admin route used by admin-web is in docs/ADMIN_ROUTES_GUIDE.md (a test enforces it).
3. Anything that needs a phone, EAS, a store or a browser stays NOT TESTED here and is not counted as done.
4. When the owner says START or NEXT, the next 25 come from this list, in order, then from Set 4 when it arrives.
Source of each item: the real code (routes the server already has but admin-web has no screen for), the "PARTIAL in code" items from B297, and gaps found by reading the files. Numbers M4578 to M4580 were used in B326.

## B333 — V5.99.87 — YaayzAdvert lifecycle and input hardening
**Theme:** Harden the newly connected YaayzAdvert vendor/moderation boundary without introducing a second advertising system or touching phone/device-only work.

M4666–M4690: **25/25 IMPLEMENTED / VALIDATED internally**.

- M4666 — verify the sealed B332 baseline and B333 identity before changes.
- M4667 — inspect the real YaayzAdvert lifecycle and preserve the existing `yaayz_ads` inventory.
- M4668 — centralize safe pagination limits for vendor and admin advert inventories.
- M4669 — reject non-integer advert page parameters instead of coercing arbitrary values.
- M4670 — keep negative offsets from reaching the database.
- M4671 — cap vendor/admin advert page size at 100.
- M4672 — preserve vendor ownership filtering on every vendor inventory read.
- M4673 — keep public Yaayz visibility restricted to active approved adverts.
- M4674 — cap inline image data to 1.5 MB before it reaches advert storage.
- M4675 — retain HTTPS-only external advert destinations.
- M4676 — reject overlong destination values explicitly.
- M4677 — keep advertiser/title/body length bounds in the normalization boundary.
- M4678 — retain HTTPS/data-image allowlisting for advert images.
- M4679 — define the valid moderation lifecycle transitions in one authority.
- M4680 — allow pending adverts to move only to approved or rejected.
- M4681 — allow approved adverts to move only to taken-down.
- M4682 — allow rejected and taken-down adverts to return only to approved.
- M4683 — refuse invalid moderation transitions with HTTP 409 semantics.
- M4684 — preserve moderation status, actor and review timestamp on every valid decision.
- M4685 — preserve mandatory reasons for rejection and takedown.
- M4686 — preserve existing owner/admin moderation permission boundaries.
- M4687 — preserve vendor review notes without exposing admin-only owner email data to vendors.
- M4688 — preserve approved-only search/public inventory behavior.
- M4689 — add a focused 25-check regression contract for the hardened boundary.
- M4690 — synchronize contract verification, roadmap, version notes, architecture decision and release handoff.

**Validation:** B333 focused contract **25/25 PASS**; cumulative contract suite **PASS**; admin-web suite **52/52 PASS**; changed JavaScript syntax **PASS**; Devil’s Walk **0 blockers**. Full dependency-backed server runtime, real database, phone/browser/device, Playwright, EAS, stores and external providers remain NOT TESTED.

**Carry-forward:** phone-only checks, real typecheck and other environment/device-only items remain excluded; no new internal blocker was found.

### B512 implementation evidence
- Zeeza referral tokens are stored as one-way hashes in a separate append-only token table, so later link generation does not invalidate older shared links.
- Referral campaigns start at profile referral-link generation and use a fixed 30-day end time. Qualified referral counts require attribution plus an activation event.
- Vendor reward review threshold: 100 qualified activations in the campaign window. User AAMI Rewards unlock threshold: 20 qualified activations; subscription-month review requires 30 additional activations in the window after the baseline (or 50 total in-window).
- Admin follow-up API and screen list user/vendor campaigns. Reward is pending review; no subscription credit mutation is performed.
- Popup first-load logic can display a recent eligible notification, extends visibility to five seconds, and deduplicates IDs. Dismissal does not mark read.
- Kitchen Hub route and settlement were not changed.

### B513 implementation evidence
- Hardened restore-to-new-path to verify the copied file size and SHA-256 after an exclusive copy; cleanup removes a failed copy only when this invocation created it, avoiding deletion of a competing process's target.
- Hardened restore drills to reject missing inputs and symlink scratch directories, use high-entropy scratch names, report generic failure details, and attempt cleanup without touching the live database.
- Added `scripts/zeeza-admin-ai-feed.mjs`, a read-only adapter using the existing admin-authorized referral-followup and notification APIs. It bounds response sizes, filters relevant notification categories, sanitizes text, requires HTTPS except loopback, and fails closed on authorization or shape errors.
- Added an AI operating prompt/handoff that defines allowed review actions and forbids reward approval, subscription credits, attribution changes, account mutation, outbound messages and secret export.
- Added focused feed, recovery and 25-milestone contract tests. Also fixed popup burst-loss by queueing recent eligible notifications oldest-first in a bounded queue, advancing after timeout/dismiss/open, and keeping dismiss separate from the server read receipt. Kitchen Hub routes and settlement logic were not changed.
- Live admin API, real admin session provisioning, watch process supervision, approved third-party AI processor, production database restore and device/browser acceptance remain untested or parked.

M7780 — Main: Let the Administrative Director appoint a Manager for each unit; a Manager's scope is that unit only — Status: PARTIAL — owner decided (2026-10-27): the Administrative Director approves a Manager's appointment and names the unit (Business Hub, Kitchen or Blaca Essentials) from a fixed list. `server/staffManagers.js` (V6.2.44 B590): the Director appoints and approves in one act; no other tier can; no self-appointment; a reason is required; one live appointment per account; an account that already works as staff cannot be appointed and a Manager cannot be made staff; the Manager's unit is read only from the appointment; the Manager's own staff requests are forced into their unit and boss, capped to the unit's permissions, and the Director still approves them; suspend, restore and remove reuse the boss hold and boss removal so the Manager's staff stop at once; audit in `staff_manager_audit`. 16 tests on a real SQLite engine; 9 of 10 deliberate breakages caught (the tenth is a second safety layer the unit rule already covers). NOT BUILT: HTTP routes, any screen, the sign-in door for Manager staff, events to Admin or Central for appointments, and a check in the general staff-request path that refuses a Manager there (only the Manager path checks). UPDATE (V6.2.47 B593): the Director can now list and decide a Manager's pending staff requests over HTTP (`GET /api/staff-links/pending`, `POST /api/staff-links/:id/decision`), resolved from an active Admin AAMI grant in Main. STILL NOT BUILT: HTTP routes to appoint, suspend, restore or remove a Manager, so no Manager can be appointed over HTTP yet. NOT TESTED over HTTP or on a device. Owner decided (2026-10-27): a unit can have more than one Manager, and Kitchen and Blaca Essentials staff are Manager staff; both are covered by tests (two Managers in one unit keep separate staff; suspending or removing one does not touch the other's staff).
16. M7781 — Main: Cascade suspension and removal: suspending a boss pauses their staff links, removing staff never deletes the NEXA account, and affected staff sessions end immediately — Status: PARTIAL — TESTED/PASSED in the module on a real SQLite engine (V6.2.41 B587): suspend, resume (approver only), remove, boss hold and restore, boss removal. Staff authority ends at once because every staff check reads the link and hold live. The NEXA account is never touched. NOT BUILT: HTTP routes, ending a staff-specific session (none exists; the personal NEXA login stays), cascade through a Manager under a held Director.

Door route rate limit (30 per 15 min per user) checked in source only. No live HTTP, no per-account lockout.

Not run on a real database. Old Manager-tier powers are not migrated. FINDING still open: `POST /api/mall/vendor/staff/accounts` still creates a staff account with a password; not retired.

M7785 — Main: Send minimized signed staff-link events from Main to Admin and Central; refuse unsigned, stale and replayed events — Status: PARTIAL — signer and verifier tested on a real SQLite engine (V6.2.42 B588): events carry only link id, NEXA account id, boss, unit and status (no permissions, codes or credentials); unsigned, bad-signature, unknown-key, stale (over 5 min), future (over 60 s), wrong-target, extra-field and replayed events are refused; 14 tests in `server/staffLinkEvents.contract.test.js`. NOT BUILT: sending from Main (no outbox, no HTTP call), and Admin/Central receivers are unchanged. NOT TESTED against live Admin or Central. UPDATE (V6.2.43 B589): `server/staffLinkOutbox.js` records what Admin and Central must be told (link id, type, target only), queues waiting news once, leases items so two senders never send the same one, backs off, parks an item as dead after 6 tries, and signs a fresh event each time it is handed out so a retry is never stale; 12 tests on a real SQLite engine, 6 deliberate breakages caught. NOT BUILT: the sender (no HTTP call is made), calling enqueue when a link changes, and any Admin or Central receiver. NOT TESTED against live Admin or Central.
